===== M0865 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.FunctionType.setPrototypeBasedOn(ObjectType,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/FunctionType.java
Original range: 341-367
Documentation:

Method code:
341 |   void setPrototypeBasedOn(ObjectType baseType, Node propertyNode) {
342 |     // This is a bit weird. We need to successfully handle these
343 |     // two cases:
344 |     // Foo.prototype = new Bar();
345 |     // and
346 |     // Foo.prototype = {baz: 3};
347 |     // In the first case, we do not want new properties to get
348 |     // added to Bar. In the second case, we do want new properties
349 |     // to get added to the type of the anonymous object.
350 |     //
351 |     // We handle this by breaking it into two cases:
352 |     //
353 |     // In the first case, we create a new PrototypeObjectType and set
354 |     // its implicit prototype to the type being assigned. This ensures
355 |     // that Bar will not get any properties of Foo.prototype, but properties
356 |     // later assigned to Bar will get inherited properly.
357 |     //
358 |     // In the second case, we just use the anonymous object as the prototype.
359 |     if (baseType.hasReferenceName() ||
360 |         isNativeObjectType() ||
361 |         baseType.isFunctionPrototypeType()) {
362 | 
363 |       baseType = new PrototypeObjectType(
364 |           registry, this.getReferenceName() + ".prototype", baseType);
365 |     }
366 |     setPrototype(baseType, propertyNode);
367 |   }

===== M0867 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.UnionTypeBuilder.addAlternate(JSType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/UnionTypeBuilder.java
Original range: 121-212
Documentation:
/**
   * Adds an alternate to the union type under construction. Returns this
   * for easy chaining.
   */
Method code:
121 |   UnionTypeBuilder addAlternate(JSType alternate) {
122 |     // build() returns the bottom type by default, so we can
123 |     // just bail out early here.
124 |     if (alternate.isNoType()) {
125 |       return this;
126 |     }
127 | 
128 |     isAllType = isAllType || alternate.isAllType();
129 | 
130 |     boolean isAlternateUnknown = alternate instanceof UnknownType;
131 |     isNativeUnknownType = isNativeUnknownType || isAlternateUnknown;
132 |     if (isAlternateUnknown) {
133 |       areAllUnknownsChecked = areAllUnknownsChecked &&
134 |           alternate.isCheckedUnknownType();
135 |     }
136 |     if (!isAllType && !isNativeUnknownType) {
137 |       if (alternate.isUnionType()) {
138 |         UnionType union = alternate.toMaybeUnionType();
139 |         for (JSType unionAlt : union.getAlternates()) {
140 |           addAlternate(unionAlt);
141 |         }
142 |       } else {
143 |         if (alternates.size() > maxUnionSize) {
144 |           return this;
145 |         }
146 | 
147 |         // Function types are special, because they have their
148 |         // own bizarro sub-lattice. See the commants on
149 |         // FunctionType#supAndInf helper and above at functionTypePosition.
150 |         if (alternate.isFunctionType() && functionTypePosition != -1) {
151 |           // See the comments on functionTypePosition above.
152 |           FunctionType other =
153 |               alternates.get(functionTypePosition).toMaybeFunctionType();
154 |           FunctionType supremum =
155 |               alternate.toMaybeFunctionType().supAndInfHelper(other, true);
156 |           alternates.set(functionTypePosition, supremum);
157 |           result = null;
158 |           return this;
159 |         }
160 | 
161 |         // Look through the alternates we've got so far,
162 |         // and check if any of them are duplicates of
163 |         // one another.
164 |         int currentIndex = 0;
165 |         Iterator<JSType> it = alternates.iterator();
166 |         while (it.hasNext()) {
167 |           JSType current = it.next();
168 | 
169 |           // Unknown and NoResolved types may just be names that haven't
170 |           // been resolved yet. So keep these in the union, and just use
171 |           // equality checking for simple de-duping.
172 |           if (alternate.isUnknownType() ||
173 |               current.isUnknownType() ||
174 |               alternate.isNoResolvedType() ||
175 |               current.isNoResolvedType()) {
176 |             if (alternate.isEquivalentTo(current)) {
177 |               // Alternate is unnecessary.
178 |               return this;
179 |             }
180 |           } else {
181 |             if (alternate.isSubtype(current)) {
182 |               // Alternate is unnecessary.
183 |               return this;
184 |             } else if (current.isSubtype(alternate)) {
185 |               // Alternate makes current obsolete
186 |               it.remove();
187 | 
188 |               if (currentIndex == functionTypePosition) {
189 |                 functionTypePosition = -1;
190 |               } else if (currentIndex < functionTypePosition) {
191 |                 functionTypePosition--;
192 |                 currentIndex--;
193 |               }
194 |             }
195 |           }
196 |           currentIndex++;
197 |         }
198 | 
199 |         if (alternate.isFunctionType()) {
200 |           // See the comments on functionTypePosition above.
201 |           Preconditions.checkState(functionTypePosition == -1);
202 |           functionTypePosition = alternates.size();
203 |         }
204 | 
205 |         alternates.add(alternate);
206 |         result = null; // invalidate the memoized result
207 |       }
208 |     } else {
209 |       result = null;
210 |     }
211 |     return this;
212 |   }

83 |     }
84 | 
85 |     ArrowType that = (ArrowType) other;
86 | 
87 |     // This is described in Draft 2 of the ES4 spec,
88 |     // Section 3.4.7: Subtyping Function Types.
89 | 
90 |     // this.returnType <: that.returnType (covariant)
91 |     if (!this.returnType.isSubtype(that.returnType)) {
92 |       return false;
93 |     }
94 | 
95 |     // that.paramType[i] <: this.paramType[i] (contravariant)
96 |     //
97 |     // If this.paramType[i] is required,
98 |     // then that.paramType[i] is required.
99 |     //
100 |     // In theory, the "required-ness" should work in the other direction as
101 |     // well. In other words, if we have
102 |     //
103 |     // function f(number, number) {}
104 |     // function g(number) {}
105 |     //
106 |     // Then f *should* not be a subtype of g, and g *should* not be
107 |     // a subtype of f. But in practice, we do not implement it this way.
108 |     // We want to support the use case where you can pass g where f is
109 |     // expected, and pretend that g ignores the second argument.
110 |     // That way, you can have a single "no-op" function, and you don't have
111 |     // to create a new no-op function for every possible type signature.
112 |     //
113 |     // So, in this case, g < f, but f !< g
114 |     Node thisParam = parameters.getFirstChild();
115 |     Node thatParam = that.parameters.getFirstChild();
116 |     while (thisParam != null && thatParam != null) {
117 |       JSType thisParamType = thisParam.getJSType();
118 |       JSType thatParamType = thatParam.getJSType();
119 |       if (thisParamType != null) {
120 |         if (thatParamType == null ||
121 |             !thatParamType.isSubtype(thisParamType)) {
122 |           return false;
123 |         }
124 |       }
125 | 
126 |       boolean thisIsVarArgs = thisParam.isVarArgs();
127 |       boolean thatIsVarArgs = thatParam.isVarArgs();
128 |       boolean thisIsOptional = thisIsVarArgs || thisParam.isOptionalArg();
129 |       boolean thatIsOptional = thatIsVarArgs || thatParam.isOptionalArg();
130 | 
131 |       // "that" can't be a supertype, because it's missing a required argument.
132 |       if (!thisIsOptional && thatIsOptional) {
133 |         // NOTE(nicksantos): In our type system, we use {function(...?)} and
134 |         // {function(...NoType)} to to indicate that arity should not be
135 |         // checked. Strictly speaking, this is not a correct formulation,
136 |         // because now a sub-function can required arguments that are var_args
137 |         // in the super-function. So we special-case this.
138 |         boolean isTopFunction =
139 |             thatIsVarArgs &&
140 |             (thatParamType == null ||
141 |              thatParamType.isUnknownType() ||
142 |              thatParamType.isNoType());
143 |         if (!isTopFunction) {
144 |           return false;
145 |         }
146 |       }
147 | 
148 |       // don't advance if we have variable arguments
149 |       if (!thisIsVarArgs) {
150 |         thisParam = thisParam.getNext();
151 |       }
152 |       if (!thatIsVarArgs) {
153 |         thatParam = thatParam.getNext();
154 |       }
155 | 
156 |       // both var_args indicates the end
157 |       if (thisIsVarArgs && thatIsVarArgs) {
158 |         thisParam = null;
159 |         thatParam = null;
160 |       }
161 |     }
162 | 
163 |     // "that" can't be a supertype, because it's missing a required arguement.
164 |     if (thisParam != null
165 |         && !thisParam.isOptionalArg() && !thisParam.isVarArgs()
166 |         && thatParam == null) {
167 |       return false;
168 |     }
169 | 
170 |     return true;
171 |   }

234 |   public int getMinArguments() {
235 |     // NOTE(nicksantos): There are some native functions that have optional

   * Infer the role of the function (whether it's a constructor or interface)
   * and what it inherits from in JSDocInfo.
   */
Method code:
324 |   FunctionTypeBuilder inferInheritance(@Nullable JSDocInfo info) {
325 |     if (info != null) {

348 |           if (maybeInterType != null &&

354 |         reportWarning(IMPLEMENTS_WITHOUT_CONSTRUCTOR, fnName);

364 |             extendedInterfaces.add((ObjectType) maybeInterfaceType);
365 |           }

371 |   }

Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.ArrowType.hasEqualParameters(ArrowType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/ArrowType.java
Original range: 177-200
Documentation:
/**
   * @return True if our parameter spec is equal to {@code that}'s parameter
   *     spec.
   */
Method code:
177 |   boolean hasEqualParameters(ArrowType that) {
178 |     Node thisParam = parameters.getFirstChild();
179 |     Node otherParam = that.parameters.getFirstChild();
180 |     while (thisParam != null && otherParam != null) {
181 |       JSType thisParamType = thisParam.getJSType();
182 |       JSType otherParamType = otherParam.getJSType();
183 |       if (thisParamType != null) {
184 |         // Both parameter lists give a type for this param, it should be equal
185 |         if (otherParamType != null &&
186 |             !thisParamType.isEquivalentTo(otherParamType)) {
187 |           return false;
188 |         }
189 |       } else {
190 |         if (otherParamType != null) {
191 |           return false;
192 |         }
193 |       }
194 |       thisParam = thisParam.getNext();
195 |       otherParam = otherParam.getNext();
196 |     }
197 |     // One of the parameters is null, so the types are only equal if both
198 |     // parameter lists are null (they are equal).
199 |     return thisParam == otherParam;
200 |   }

===== M0911 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeInference.inferPropertyTypesToMatchConstraint(JSType,JSType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeInference.java
Original range: 1113-1124
Documentation:
/**
   * Suppose X is an object with inferred properties.
   * Suppose also that X is used in a way where it would only type-check
   * correctly if some of those properties are widened.
   * Then we should be polite and automatically widen X's properties for him.
   *
   * For a concrete example, consider:
   * param x {{prop: (number|undefined)}}
   * function f(x) {}
   * f({});
   *
   * If we give the anonymous object an inferred property of (number|undefined),
   * then this code will type-check appropriately.
   */
Method code:
1113 |   private void inferPropertyTypesToMatchConstraint(
1114 |       JSType type, JSType constraint) {
1115 |     if (type == null || constraint == null) {
1116 |       return;
1117 |     }
1118 | 
1119 |     ObjectType constraintObj =
1120 |         ObjectType.cast(constraint.restrictByNotNullOrUndefined());
1121 |     if (constraintObj != null) {
1122 |       type.matchConstraint(constraintObj);
1123 |     }
1124 |   }

528 |     for (JSType alternate : alternates) {

Original range: 164-180
Documentation:
/**
     * There are two types of calls we are interested in calls without explicit
     * "this" values (what we are call "free" calls) and direct call to eval.
     */
Method code:
164 |     private void annotateCalls(Node n) {
165 |       Preconditions.checkState(n.isCall());
166 | 
167 |       // Keep track of of the "this" context of a call.  A call without an
168 |       // explicit "this" is a free call.
169 |       Node first = n.getFirstChild();
170 |       if (!NodeUtil.isGet(first)) {
171 |         n.putBooleanProp(Node.FREE_CALL, true);
172 |       }
173 | 
174 |       // Keep track of the context in which eval is called. It is important
175 |       // to distinguish between "(0, eval)()" and "eval()".
176 |       if (first.isName() &&
177 |           "eval".equals(first.getString())) {
178 |         first.putBooleanProp(Node.DIRECT_EVAL, true);
179 |       }
180 |     }

/**
   * Declares a property on its owner, if necessary.
   * @return True if a property was declared.
   */
Method code:
577 |   private boolean ensurePropertyDeclaredHelper(
578 |       Node getprop, ObjectType objectType) {
579 |     String propName = getprop.getLastChild().getString();
580 |     String qName = getprop.getQualifiedName();
581 |     if (qName != null) {
582 |       Var var = syntacticScope.getVar(qName);
583 |       if (var != null && !var.isTypeInferred()) {
584 |         // Handle normal declarations that could not be addressed earlier.
585 |         if (propName.equals("prototype") ||
586 |         // Handle prototype declarations that could not be addressed earlier.
587 |             (!objectType.hasOwnProperty(propName) &&

589 |                  (var.isExtern() && !objectType.isNativeObjectType())))) {
590 |           return objectType.defineDeclaredProperty(
591 |               propName, var.getType(), getprop);
592 |         }
593 |       }
594 |     }
595 |     return false;
596 |   }

===== M0945 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.parsing.IRFactory.TransformDispatcher.processObjectLiteral(ObjectLiteral)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/parsing/IRFactory.java
Original range: 794-831
Documentation:

Method code:
794 |     @Override
795 |     Node processObjectLiteral(ObjectLiteral literalNode) {
796 |       if (literalNode.isDestructuring()) {
797 |         reportDestructuringAssign(literalNode);
798 |       }
799 | 
800 |       Node node = newNode(Token.OBJECTLIT);
801 |       for (ObjectProperty el : literalNode.getElements()) {
802 |         if (config.languageMode == LanguageMode.ECMASCRIPT3) {
803 |           if (el.isGetter()) {
804 |             reportGetter(el);
805 |             continue;
806 |           } else if (el.isSetter()) {
807 |             reportSetter(el);
808 |             continue;
809 |           }
810 |         }
811 | 
812 |         Node key = transformAsString(el.getLeft());
813 |         Node value = transform(el.getRight());
814 |         if (el.isGetter()) {
815 |           key.setType(Token.GETTER_DEF);
816 |           Preconditions.checkState(value.isFunction());
817 |           if (getFnParamNode(value).hasChildren()) {
818 |             reportGetterParam(el.getLeft());
819 |           }
820 |         } else if (el.isSetter()) {
821 |           key.setType(Token.SETTER_DEF);
822 |           Preconditions.checkState(value.isFunction());
823 |           if (!getFnParamNode(value).hasOneChild()) {
824 |             reportSetterParam(el.getLeft());
825 |           }
826 |         }
827 |         key.addChildToFront(value);
828 |         node.addChildToBack(key);
829 |       }
830 |       return node;
831 |     }

===== M0949 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeValidator.expectNotNullOrUndefined(NodeTraversal,Node,JSType,String,JSType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeValidator.java
Original range: 241-267
Documentation:
/**
   * Expect the type to be anything but the null or void type. If the
   * expectation is not met, issue a warning at the provided node's
   * source code position. Note that a union type that includes the
   * void type and at least one other type meets the expectation.
   * @return Whether the expectation was met.
   */
Method code:
241 |   boolean expectNotNullOrUndefined(
242 |       NodeTraversal t, Node n, JSType type, String msg, JSType expectedType) {
243 |     if (!type.isNoType() && !type.isUnknownType() &&
244 |         type.isSubtype(nullOrUndefined) &&
245 |         !containsForwardDeclaredUnresolvedName(type)) {
246 | 
247 |       // There's one edge case right now that we don't handle well, and
248 |       // that we don't want to warn about.
249 |       // if (this.x == null) {
250 |       //   this.initializeX();
251 |       //   this.x.foo();
252 |       // }
253 |       // In this case, we incorrectly type x because of how we
254 |       // infer properties locally. See issue 109.
255 |       // http://code.google.com/p/closure-compiler/issues/detail?id=109
256 |       //
257 |       // We do not do this inference globally.
258 |       if (n.isGetProp() &&
259 |           !t.inGlobalScope() && type.isNullType()) {
260 |         return true;
261 |       }
262 | 
263 |       mismatch(t, n, msg, type, expectedType);
264 |       return false;
265 |     }
266 |     return true;
267 |   }

===== M0962 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeInference.traverseName(Node,FlowScope)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeInference.java
Original range: 598-637
Documentation:

Method code:
598 |   private FlowScope traverseName(Node n, FlowScope scope) {
599 |     String varName = n.getString();
600 |     Node value = n.getFirstChild();
601 |     JSType type = n.getJSType();
602 |     if (value != null) {
603 |       scope = traverse(value, scope);
604 |       updateScopeForTypeChange(scope, n, n.getJSType() /* could be null */,
605 |           getJSType(value));
606 |       return scope;
607 |     } else {
608 |       StaticSlot<JSType> var = scope.getSlot(varName);
609 |       if (var != null) {
610 |         // There are two situations where we don't want to use type information
611 |         // from the scope, even if we have it.
612 | 
613 |         // 1) The var is escaped in a weird way, e.g.,
614 |         // function f() { var x = 3; function g() { x = null } (x); }
615 |         boolean isInferred = var.isTypeInferred();
616 |         boolean unflowable = isInferred &&
617 |             isUnflowable(syntacticScope.getVar(varName));
618 | 
619 |         // 2) We're reading type information from another scope for an
620 |         // inferred variable.
621 |         // var t = null; function f() { (t); }
622 |         boolean nonLocalInferredSlot =
623 |             isInferred &&
624 |             syntacticScope.getParent() != null &&
625 |             var == syntacticScope.getParent().getSlot(varName);
626 | 
627 |         if (!unflowable && !nonLocalInferredSlot) {
628 |           type = var.getType();
629 |           if (type == null) {
630 |             type = getNativeType(UNKNOWN_TYPE);
631 |           }
632 |         }
633 |       }
634 |     }
635 |     n.setJSType(type);
636 |     return scope;
637 |   }

===== M0965 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.LinkedFlowScope.equals(Object)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/LinkedFlowScope.java
Original range: 256-306
Documentation:

Method code:
256 |   @Override
257 |   public boolean equals(Object other) {
258 |     if (other instanceof LinkedFlowScope) {
259 |       LinkedFlowScope that = (LinkedFlowScope) other;
260 |       if (this.optimize() == that.optimize()) {
261 |         return true;
262 |       }
263 | 
264 |       // If two flow scopes are in the same function, then they could have
265 |       // two possible function scopes: the real one and the BOTTOM scope.
266 |       // If they have different function scopes, we *should* iterate thru all
267 |       // the variables in each scope and compare. However, 99.9% of the time,
268 |       // they're not equal. And the other .1% of the time, we can pretend
269 |       // they're equal--this just means that data flow analysis will have
270 |       // to propagate the entry lattice a little bit further than it
271 |       // really needs to. Everything will still come out ok.
272 |       if (this.getFunctionScope() != that.getFunctionScope()) {
273 |         return false;
274 |       }
275 | 
276 |       if (cache == that.cache) {
277 |         // If the two flow scopes have the same cache, then we can check
278 |         // equality a lot faster: by just looking at the "dirty" elements
279 |         // in the cache, and comparing them in both scopes.
280 |         for (String name : cache.dirtySymbols) {
281 |           if (diffSlots(getSlot(name), that.getSlot(name))) {
282 |             return false;
283 |           }
284 |         }
285 | 
286 |         return true;
287 |       }
288 | 
289 |       Map<String, StaticSlot<JSType>> myFlowSlots = allFlowSlots();
290 |       Map<String, StaticSlot<JSType>> otherFlowSlots = that.allFlowSlots();
291 | 
292 |       for (StaticSlot<JSType> slot : myFlowSlots.values()) {
293 |         if (diffSlots(slot, otherFlowSlots.get(slot.getName()))) {
294 |           return false;
295 |         }
296 |         otherFlowSlots.remove(slot.getName());
297 |       }
298 |       for (StaticSlot<JSType> slot : otherFlowSlots.values()) {
299 |         if (diffSlots(slot, myFlowSlots.get(slot.getName()))) {
300 |           return false;
301 |         }
302 |       }
303 |       return true;
304 |     }
305 |     return false;
306 |   }

/**
     * Look for class-defining calls.
     * Because JS has no 'native' syntax for defining classes,
     * this is often very coding-convention dependent and business-logic heavy.
     */
Method code:
1332 |     private void checkForClassDefiningCalls(
1333 |         NodeTraversal t, Node n, Node parent) {
1334 |       SubclassRelationship relationship =
1335 |           codingConvention.getClassesDefinedByCall(n);
1336 |       if (relationship != null) {
1337 |         FunctionType superCtor = getFunctionType(
1338 |             scope.getVar(relationship.superclassName));
1339 |         FunctionType subCtor = getFunctionType(
1340 |             scope.getVar(relationship.subclassName));
1341 |         if (superCtor != null && superCtor.isConstructor() &&
1342 |             subCtor != null && subCtor.isConstructor()) {
1343 |           ObjectType superClass = superCtor.getInstanceType();
1344 |           ObjectType subClass = subCtor.getInstanceType();
1345 | 
1346 |           // superCtor and subCtor might be structural constructors
1347 |           // (like {function(new:Object)}) so we need to resolve them back
1348 |           // to the original ctor objects.
1349 |           superCtor = superClass.getConstructor();
1350 |           subCtor = subClass.getConstructor();
1351 | 
1352 |           if (relationship.type == SubclassType.INHERITS &&
1353 |               !superClass.isEmptyType() && !subClass.isEmptyType()) {
1354 |             validator.expectSuperType(t, n, superClass, subClass);
1355 |           }
1356 | 
1357 |           if (superCtor != null && subCtor != null) {
1358 |             codingConvention.applySubclassRelationship(
1359 |                 superCtor, subCtor, relationship.type);
1360 |           }
1361 |         }
1362 |       }
1363 | 
1364 |       String singletonGetterClassName =
1365 |           codingConvention.getSingletonGetterClassName(n);
1366 |       if (singletonGetterClassName != null) {
1367 |         ObjectType objectType = ObjectType.cast(
1368 |             typeRegistry.getType(singletonGetterClassName));
1369 |         if (objectType != null) {
1370 |           FunctionType functionType = objectType.getConstructor();
1371 | 
1372 |           if (functionType != null) {
1373 |             FunctionType getterType =
1374 |                 typeRegistry.createFunctionType(objectType);
1375 |             codingConvention.applySingletonGetter(functionType, getterType,
1376 |                 objectType);
1377 |           }
1378 |         }
1379 |       }
1380 | 
1381 |       DelegateRelationship delegateRelationship =
1382 |           codingConvention.getDelegateRelationship(n);
1383 |       if (delegateRelationship != null) {
1384 |         applyDelegateRelationship(delegateRelationship);
1385 |       }
1386 | 
1387 |       ObjectLiteralCast objectLiteralCast =
1388 |           codingConvention.getObjectLiteralCast(t, n);
1389 |       if (objectLiteralCast != null) {
1390 |         ObjectType type = ObjectType.cast(
1391 |             typeRegistry.getType(objectLiteralCast.typeName));
1392 |         if (type != null && type.getConstructor() != null) {
1393 |           setDeferredType(objectLiteralCast.objectNode, type);
1394 |         } else {

/**
   * This function takes a set of GETPROP nodes and produces a string that is
   * each property separated by dots. If the node ultimately under the left
   * sub-tree is not a simple name, this is not a valid qualified name.
   *
   * @return a null if this is not a qualified name, or a dot-separated string
   *         of the name and properties.
   */
Method code:
1565 |   public String getQualifiedName() {
1566 |     if (type == Token.NAME) {
1567 |       return getString();
1568 |     } else if (type == Token.GETPROP) {
1569 |       String left = getFirstChild().getQualifiedName();
1570 |       if (left == null) {
1571 |         return null;
1572 |       }
1573 |       return left + "." + getLastChild().getString();
1574 |     } else if (type == Token.THIS) {
1575 |       return "this";
1576 |     } else {
1577 |       return null;
1578 |     }
1579 |   }

===== M0976 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.process(Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 148-190
Documentation:

Method code:
148 |   @Override
149 |   public void process(Node externs, Node root) {
150 |     this.root = root;
151 |     astPositionCounter = 0;
152 |     astPosition = Maps.newHashMap();
153 |     nodePriorities = Maps.newHashMap();
154 |     cfg = new AstControlFlowGraph(computeFallThrough(root), nodePriorities,
155 |                                   edgeAnnotations);
156 |     NodeTraversal.traverse(compiler, root, this);
157 |     astPosition.put(null, ++astPositionCounter); // the implicit return is last.
158 | 
159 |     // Now, generate the priority of nodes by doing a depth-first
160 |     // search on the CFG.
161 |     priorityCounter = 0;
162 |     DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> entry = cfg.getEntry();
163 |     prioritizeFromEntryNode(entry);
164 | 
165 |     if (shouldTraverseFunctions) {
166 |       // If we're traversing inner functions, we need to rank the
167 |       // priority of them too.
168 |       for (DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> candidate : cfg.getDirectedGraphNodes()) {
169 |         Node value = candidate.getValue();
170 |         if (value != null && value.isFunction()) {
171 |           Preconditions.checkState(
172 |               !nodePriorities.containsKey(candidate) || candidate == entry);
173 |           prioritizeFromEntryNode(candidate);
174 |         }
175 |       }
176 |     }
177 | 
178 |     // At this point, all reachable nodes have been given a priority, but
179 |     // unreachable nodes have not been given a priority. Put them last.
180 |     // Presumably, it doesn't really matter what priority they get, since
181 |     // this shouldn't happen in real code.
182 |     for (DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> candidate : cfg.getDirectedGraphNodes()) {
183 |       if (!nodePriorities.containsKey(candidate)) {
184 |         nodePriorities.put(candidate, ++priorityCounter);
185 |       }
186 |     }

===== M0977 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.parsing.IRFactory.TransformDispatcher.processStringLiteral(StringLiteral)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/parsing/IRFactory.java
Original range: 898-925
Documentation:

Method code:
898 |     @Override
899 |     Node processStringLiteral(StringLiteral literalNode) {
900 |       String value = literalNode.getValue();
901 |       Node n = newStringNode(value);
902 |       if (value.indexOf('\u000B') != -1) {
903 |         // NOTE(nicksantos): In JavaScript, there are 3 ways to
904 |         // represent a vertical tab: \v, \x0B, \u000B.
905 |         // The \v notation was added later, and is not understood
906 |         // on IE. So we need to preserve it as-is. This is really
907 |         // obnoxious, because we do not have a good way to represent
908 |         // how the original string was encoded without making the
909 |         // representation of strings much more complicated.
910 |         //
911 |         // To handle this, we look at the original source test, and
912 |         // mark the string as \v-encoded or not. If a string is
913 |         // \v encoded, then all the vertical tabs in that string
914 |         // will be encoded with a \v.
915 |         int start = literalNode.getAbsolutePosition();
916 |         int end = start + literalNode.getLength();
917 |         if (start < sourceString.length() &&
918 |             (sourceString.substring(
919 |                  start, Math.min(sourceString.length(), end))
920 |              .indexOf("\\v") != -1)) {
921 |           n.putBooleanProp(Node.SLASH_V, true);
922 |         }
923 |       }
924 |       return n;
925 |     }

/**
   * TopLevelTypeExpression := TypeExpression
   *     | TypeUnionList
   *
   * We made this rule up, for the sake of backwards compatibility.
   */
Method code:
1618 |   private Node parseTopLevelTypeExpression(JsDocToken token) {
1619 |     Node typeExpr = parseTypeExpression(token);
1620 |     if (typeExpr != null) {
1621 |       // top-level unions are allowed
1622 |       if (match(JsDocToken.PIPE)) {
1623 |         next();
1624 |         if (match(JsDocToken.PIPE)) {
1625 |           // We support double pipes for backwards-compatibility.
1626 |           next();
1627 |         }
1628 |         skipEOLs();
1629 |         token = next();
1630 |         return parseUnionTypeWithAlternate(token, typeExpr);
1631 |       }

===== M0989 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.parsing.JsDocInfoParser.parseParamTypeExpressionAnnotation(JsDocToken)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/parsing/JsDocInfoParser.java
Original range: 1553-1588
Documentation:
/**
   * ParamTypeExpressionAnnotation :=
   *     '{' OptionalParameterType '}' |
   *     '{' TopLevelTypeExpression '}' |
   *     '{' '...' TopLevelTypeExpression '}'
   *
   * OptionalParameterType :=
   *     TopLevelTypeExpression '='
   */
Method code:
1553 |   private Node parseParamTypeExpressionAnnotation(JsDocToken token) {
1554 |     Preconditions.checkArgument(token == JsDocToken.LC);
1555 | 
1556 |     skipEOLs();
1557 | 
1558 |     boolean restArg = false;
1559 |     token = next();
1560 |     if (token == JsDocToken.ELLIPSIS) {
1561 |       token = next();
1562 |       if (token == JsDocToken.RC) {
1563 |         // EMPTY represents the UNKNOWN type in the Type AST.
1564 |         return wrapNode(Token.ELLIPSIS, IR.empty());
1565 |       }
1566 |       restArg = true;
1567 |     }
1568 | 
1569 |     Node typeNode = parseTopLevelTypeExpression(token);
1570 |     if (typeNode != null) {
1571 |       skipEOLs();
1572 |       if (restArg) {
1573 |         typeNode = wrapNode(Token.ELLIPSIS, typeNode);
1574 |       } else if (match(JsDocToken.EQUALS)) {
1575 |         next();
1576 |         skipEOLs();
1577 |         typeNode = wrapNode(Token.EQUALS, typeNode);
1578 |       }
1579 | 
1580 |       if (!match(JsDocToken.RC)) {
1581 |         reportTypeSyntaxWarning("msg.jsdoc.missing.rc");
1582 |       } else {
1583 |         next();
1584 |       }
1585 |     }
1586 | 
1587 |     return typeNode;
1588 |   }

===== M1007 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.FunctionTypeBuilder.inferParameterTypes(Node,JSDocInfo)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/FunctionTypeBuilder.java
Original range: 426-505
Documentation:
/**
   * Infer the parameter types from the list of argument names and
   * the doc info.
   */
Method code:
426 |   FunctionTypeBuilder inferParameterTypes(@Nullable Node argsParent,
427 |       @Nullable JSDocInfo info) {
428 |     if (argsParent == null) {
429 |       if (info == null) {
430 |         return this;
431 |       } else {
432 |         return inferParameterTypes(info);
433 |       }
434 |     }
435 | 
436 |     // arguments
437 |     Node oldParameterType = null;
438 |     if (parametersNode != null) {
439 |       oldParameterType = parametersNode.getFirstChild();
440 |     }
441 | 
442 |     FunctionParamBuilder builder = new FunctionParamBuilder(typeRegistry);
443 |     boolean warnedAboutArgList = false;
444 |     Set<String> allJsDocParams = (info == null) ?
445 |         Sets.<String>newHashSet() :
446 |         Sets.newHashSet(info.getParameterNames());
447 |     boolean foundTemplateType = false;
448 |     boolean isVarArgs = false;
449 |     for (Node arg : argsParent.children()) {
450 |       String argumentName = arg.getString();
451 |       allJsDocParams.remove(argumentName);
452 | 
453 |       // type from JSDocInfo
454 |       JSType parameterType = null;
455 |       boolean isOptionalParam = isOptionalParameter(arg, info);
456 |       isVarArgs = isVarArgsParameter(arg, info);
457 | 
458 |       if (info != null && info.hasParameterType(argumentName)) {
459 |         parameterType =
460 |             info.getParameterType(argumentName).evaluate(scope, typeRegistry);
461 |       } else if (oldParameterType != null &&
462 |           oldParameterType.getJSType() != null) {
463 |         parameterType = oldParameterType.getJSType();
464 |         isOptionalParam = oldParameterType.isOptionalArg();
465 |         isVarArgs = oldParameterType.isVarArgs();
466 |       } else {
467 |         parameterType = typeRegistry.getNativeType(UNKNOWN_TYPE);
468 |       }
469 | 
470 |       if (templateTypeName != null &&
471 |           parameterType.restrictByNotNullOrUndefined().isTemplateType()) {
472 |         if (foundTemplateType) {
473 |           reportError(TEMPLATE_TYPE_DUPLICATED, fnName);
474 |         }
475 |         foundTemplateType = true;
476 |       }
477 |       warnedAboutArgList |= addParameter(
478 |           builder, parameterType, warnedAboutArgList,
479 |           isOptionalParam,
480 |           isVarArgs);
481 | 
482 |       if (oldParameterType != null) {
483 |         oldParameterType = oldParameterType.getNext();
484 |       }
485 |     }
486 | 
487 |     // Copy over any old parameters that aren't in the param list.
488 |     if (!isVarArgs) {
489 |       while (oldParameterType != null && !isVarArgs) {
490 |         builder.newParameterFromNode(oldParameterType);
491 |         oldParameterType = oldParameterType.getNext();
492 |       }
493 |     }
494 | 
495 |     if (templateTypeName != null && !foundTemplateType) {
496 |       reportError(TEMPLATE_TYPE_EXPECTED, fnName);
497 |     }
498 | 
499 |     for (String inexistentName : allJsDocParams) {
500 |       reportWarning(INEXISTANT_PARAM, inexistentName, fnName);
501 |     }
502 | 
503 |     parametersNode = builder.build();
504 |     return this;
505 |   }

===== M1018 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.FunctionTypeBuilder.getOrCreateConstructor()
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/FunctionTypeBuilder.java
Original range: 662-698
Documentation:
/**
   * Returns a constructor function either by returning it from the
   * registry if it exists or creating and registering a new type. If
   * there is already a type, then warn if the existing type is
   * different than the one we are creating, though still return the
   * existing function if possible.  The primary purpose of this is
   * that registering a constructor will fail for all built-in types
   * that are initialized in {@link JSTypeRegistry}.  We a) want to
   * make sure that the type information specified in the externs file
   * matches what is in the registry and b) annotate the externs with
   * the {@link JSType} from the registry so that there are not two
   * separate JSType objects for one type.
   */
Method code:
662 |   private FunctionType getOrCreateConstructor() {
663 |     FunctionType fnType = typeRegistry.createConstructorType(
664 |         fnName, contents.getSourceNode(), parametersNode, returnType);
665 |     JSType existingType = typeRegistry.getType(fnName);
666 | 
667 |     if (existingType != null) {
668 |       boolean isInstanceObject = existingType.isInstanceType();
669 |       if (isInstanceObject || fnName.equals("Function")) {
670 |         FunctionType existingFn =
671 |             isInstanceObject ?
672 |             existingType.toObjectType().getConstructor() :
673 |             typeRegistry.getNativeFunctionType(FUNCTION_FUNCTION_TYPE);
674 | 
675 |         if (existingFn.getSource() == null) {
676 |           existingFn.setSource(contents.getSourceNode());
677 |         }
678 | 
679 |         if (!existingFn.hasEqualCallType(fnType)) {
680 |           reportWarning(TYPE_REDEFINITION, fnName,
681 |               fnType.toString(), existingFn.toString());
682 |         }
683 | 
684 |         return existingFn;
685 |       } else {
686 |         // We fall through and return the created type, even though it will fail
687 |         // to register. We have no choice as we have to return a function. We
688 |         // issue an error elsewhere though, so the user should fix it.
689 |       }
690 |     }
691 | 
692 |     maybeSetBaseType(fnType);
693 | 
694 |     if (getScopeDeclaredIn().isGlobal() && !fnName.isEmpty()) {
695 |       typeRegistry.declareType(fnName, fnType.getInstanceType());
696 |     }
697 |     return fnType;
698 |   }

379 |         sb.append(": ");

383 |         if (!forAnnotations && i == MAX_PRETTY_PRINTED_PROPERTIES) {
384 |           sb.append(", ...");
385 |           break;
386 |         }
387 |       }
388 |

Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.NodeUtil.getBestJSDocInfo(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/NodeUtil.java
Original range: 3003-3029
Documentation:
/** Find the best JSDoc for the given node. */
Method code:
3003 |   static JSDocInfo getBestJSDocInfo(Node n) {
3004 |     JSDocInfo info = n.getJSDocInfo();
3005 |     if (info == null) {
3006 |       Node parent = n.getParent();
3007 |       if (parent == null) {
3008 |         return null;
3009 |       }
3010 | 
3011 |       if (parent.isName()) {
3012 |         return getBestJSDocInfo(parent);
3013 |       } else if (parent.isAssign()) {
3014 |         return parent.getJSDocInfo();
3015 |       } else if (isObjectLitKey(parent, parent.getParent())) {
3016 |         return parent.getJSDocInfo();
3017 |       } else if (parent.isFunction()) {
3018 |         return parent.getJSDocInfo();
3019 |       } else if (parent.isVar() && parent.hasOneChild()) {
3020 |         return parent.getJSDocInfo();
3021 |       } else if ((parent.isHook() && parent.getFirstChild() != n) ||
3022 |                  parent.isOr() ||
3023 |                  parent.isAnd() ||
3024 |                  (parent.isComma() && parent.getFirstChild() != n)) {
3025 |         return getBestJSDocInfo(parent);
3026 |       }
3027 |     }
3028 |     return info;
3029 |   }

===== M1030 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.FunctionTypeBuilder.inferFromOverriddenFunction(FunctionType,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/FunctionTypeBuilder.java
Original range: 243-301
Documentation:
/**
   * Infer the parameter and return types of a function from
   * the parameter and return types of the function it is overriding.
   *
   * @param oldType The function being overridden. Does nothing if this is null.
   * @param paramsParent The LP node of the function that we're assigning to.
   *     If null, that just means we're not initializing this to a function
   *     literal.
   */
Method code:
243 |   FunctionTypeBuilder inferFromOverriddenFunction(
244 |       @Nullable FunctionType oldType, @Nullable Node paramsParent) {
245 |     if (oldType == null) {
246 |       return this;
247 |     }
248 | 
249 |     returnType = oldType.getReturnType();
250 |     returnTypeInferred = oldType.isReturnTypeInferred();
251 |     if (paramsParent == null) {
252 |       // Not a function literal.
253 |       parametersNode = oldType.getParametersNode();
254 |       if (parametersNode == null) {
255 |         parametersNode = new FunctionParamBuilder(typeRegistry).build();
256 |       }
257 |     } else {
258 |       // We're overriding with a function literal. Apply type information
259 |       // to each parameter of the literal.
260 |       FunctionParamBuilder paramBuilder =
261 |           new FunctionParamBuilder(typeRegistry);
262 |       Iterator<Node> oldParams = oldType.getParameters().iterator();
263 |       boolean warnedAboutArgList = false;
264 |       boolean oldParamsListHitOptArgs = false;
265 |       for (Node currentParam = paramsParent.getFirstChild();
266 |            currentParam != null; currentParam = currentParam.getNext()) {
267 |         if (oldParams.hasNext()) {
268 |           Node oldParam = oldParams.next();
269 |           Node newParam = paramBuilder.newParameterFromNode(oldParam);
270 | 
271 |           oldParamsListHitOptArgs = oldParamsListHitOptArgs ||
272 |               oldParam.isVarArgs() ||
273 |               oldParam.isOptionalArg();
274 | 
275 |           // The subclass method might write its var_args as individual
276 |           // arguments.
277 |           if (currentParam.getNext() != null && newParam.isVarArgs()) {
278 |             newParam.setVarArgs(false);
279 |             newParam.setOptionalArg(true);
280 |           }
281 |         } else {
282 |           warnedAboutArgList |= addParameter(
283 |               paramBuilder,
284 |               typeRegistry.getNativeType(UNKNOWN_TYPE),
285 |               warnedAboutArgList,
286 |               codingConvention.isOptionalParameter(currentParam) ||
287 |                   oldParamsListHitOptArgs,
288 |               codingConvention.isVarArgsParameter(currentParam));
289 |         }
290 |       }
291 | 
292 |       // Clone any remaining params that aren't in the function literal,
293 |       // but make them optional.
294 |       while (oldParams.hasNext()) {
295 |         paramBuilder.newOptionalParameterFromNode(oldParams.next());
296 |       }
297 | 
298 |       parametersNode = paramBuilder.build();
299 |     }
300 |     return this;
301 |   }

===== M1033 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.FunctionType.isSubtype(JSType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/FunctionType.java
Original range: 941-983
Documentation:
/**
   * A function is a subtype of another if their call methods are related via
   * subtyping and {@code this} is a subtype of {@code that} with regard to
   * the prototype chain.
   */
Method code:
941 |   @Override
942 |   public boolean isSubtype(JSType that) {
943 |     if (JSType.isSubtypeHelper(this, that)) {
944 |       return true;
945 |     }
946 | 
947 |     if (that.isFunctionType()) {
948 |       FunctionType other = that.toMaybeFunctionType();
949 |       if (other.isInterface()) {
950 |         // Any function can be assigned to an interface function.
951 |         return true;
952 |       }
953 |       if (this.isInterface()) {
954 |         // An interface function cannot be assigned to anything.
955 |         return false;
956 |       }
957 |       // If functionA is a subtype of functionB, then their "this" types
958 |       // should be contravariant. However, this causes problems because
959 |       // of the way we enforce overrides. Because function(this:SubFoo)
960 |       // is not a subtype of function(this:Foo), our override check treats
961 |       // this as an error. It also screws up out standard method
962 |       // for aliasing constructors. Let's punt on all this for now.
963 |       // TODO(nicksantos): fix this.
964 |       boolean treatThisTypesAsCovariant =
965 |         // If either one of these is a ctor, skip 'this' checking.
966 |         this.isConstructor() || other.isConstructor() ||
967 | 
968 |         // An interface 'this'-type is non-restrictive.
969 |         // In practical terms, if C implements I, and I has a method m,
970 |         // then any m doesn't necessarily have to C#m's 'this'
971 |         // type doesn't need to match I.
972 |         (other.typeOfThis.getConstructor() != null &&
973 |              other.typeOfThis.getConstructor().isInterface()) ||
974 | 
975 |         // If one of the 'this' types is covariant of the other,
976 |         // then we'll treat them as covariant (see comment above).
977 |         other.typeOfThis.isSubtype(this.typeOfThis) ||
978 |         this.typeOfThis.isSubtype(other.typeOfThis);
979 |       return treatThisTypesAsCovariant && this.call.isSubtype(other.call);
980 |     }
981 | 
982 |     return getNativeType(JSTypeNative.FUNCTION_PROTOTYPE).isSubtype(that);
983 |   }

===== M1039 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.RecordType.isSubtype(ObjectType,RecordType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/RecordType.java
Original range: 229-268
Documentation:
/** Determines if typeA is a subtype of typeB */
Method code:
229 |   static boolean isSubtype(ObjectType typeA, RecordType typeB) {
230 |     // typeA is a subtype of record type typeB iff:
231 |     // 1) typeA has all the properties declared in typeB.
232 |     // 2) And for each property of typeB,
233 |     //    2a) if the property of typeA is declared, it must be equal
234 |     //        to the type of the property of typeB,
235 |     //    2b) otherwise, it must be a subtype of the property of typeB.
236 |     //
237 |     // To figure out why this is true, consider the following pseudo-code:
238 |     // /** @type {{a: (Object,null)}} */ var x;
239 |     // /** @type {{a: !Object}} */ var y;
240 |     // var z = {a: {}};
241 |     // x.a = null;
242 |     //
243 |     // y cannot be assigned to x, because line 4 would violate y's declared
244 |     // properties. But z can be assigned to x. Even though z and y are the
245 |     // same type, the properties of z are inferred--and so an assignment
246 |     // to the property of z would not violate any restrictions on it.
247 |     for (String property : typeB.properties.keySet()) {
248 |       if (!typeA.hasProperty(property)) {
249 |         return false;
250 |       }
251 | 
252 |       JSType propA = typeA.getPropertyType(property);
253 |       JSType propB = typeB.getPropertyType(property);
254 |       if (!propA.isUnknownType() && !propB.isUnknownType()) {
255 |         if (typeA.isPropertyTypeDeclared(property)) {
256 |           if (!propA.isEquivalentTo(propB)) {
257 |             return false;
258 |           }
259 |         } else {
260 |           if (!propA.isSubtype(propB)) {
261 |             return false;
262 |           }
263 |         }
264 |       }
265 |     }
266 | 
267 |     return true;
268 |   }

===== M1048 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.shouldTraverse(NodeTraversal,Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 217-292
Documentation:

Method code:
217 |   @Override
218 |   public boolean shouldTraverse(
219 |       NodeTraversal nodeTraversal, Node n, Node parent) {
220 |     astPosition.put(n, astPositionCounter++);
221 | 
222 |     switch (n.getType()) {
223 |       case Token.FUNCTION:
224 |         if (shouldTraverseFunctions || n == cfg.getEntry().getValue()) {
225 |           exceptionHandler.push(n);
226 |           return true;
227 |         }
228 |         return false;
229 |       case Token.TRY:
230 |         exceptionHandler.push(n);
231 |         return true;
232 |     }
233 | 
234 |     /*
235 |      * We are going to stop the traversal depending on what the node's parent
236 |      * is.
237 |      *
238 |      * We are only interested in adding edges between nodes that change control
239 |      * flow. The most obvious ones are loops and IF-ELSE's. A statement
240 |      * transfers control to its next sibling.
241 |      *
242 |      * In case of an expression tree, there is no control flow within the tree
243 |      * even when there are short circuited operators and conditionals. When we
244 |      * are doing data flow analysis, we will simply synthesize lattices up the
245 |      * expression tree by finding the meet at each expression node.
246 |      *
247 |      * For example: within a Token.SWITCH, the expression in question does not
248 |      * change the control flow and need not to be considered.
249 |      */
250 |     if (parent != null) {
251 |       switch (parent.getType()) {
252 |         case Token.FOR:
253 |           // Only traverse the body of the for loop.
254 |           return n == parent.getLastChild();
255 | 
256 |         // Skip the conditions.
257 |         case Token.IF:
258 |         case Token.WHILE:
259 |         case Token.WITH:
260 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild();
261 |         case Token.DO:
262 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild().getNext();
263 |         // Only traverse the body of the cases
264 |         case Token.SWITCH:
265 |         case Token.CASE:
266 |         case Token.CATCH:
267 |         case Token.LABEL:
268 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild();
269 |         case Token.FUNCTION:
270 |           return n == parent.getFirstChild().getNext().getNext();
271 |         case Token.CONTINUE:
272 |         case Token.BREAK:
273 |         case Token.EXPR_RESULT:
274 |         case Token.VAR:
275 |         case Token.RETURN:
276 |         case Token.THROW:
277 |           return false;
278 |         case Token.TRY:
279 |           /* Just before we are about to visit the second child of the TRY node,
280 |            * we know that we will be visiting either the CATCH or the FINALLY.
281 |            * In other words, we know that the post order traversal of the TRY
282 |            * block has been finished, no more exceptions can be caught by the
283 |            * handler at this TRY block and should be taken out of the stack.
284 |            */
285 |           if (n == parent.getFirstChild().getNext()) {
286 |             Preconditions.checkState(exceptionHandler.peek() == parent);
287 |             exceptionHandler.pop();
288 |           }
289 |       }
290 |     }
291 |     return true;
292 |   }

Method code:
288 |   public void traverseRoots(List<Node> roots) {
289 |     if (roots.isEmpty()) {
290 |       return;
291 |     }
292 | 
293 |     try {
294 |       Node scopeRoot = roots.get(0).getParent();
295 |       Preconditions.checkState(scopeRoot != null);
296 | 
297 |       inputId = NodeUtil.getInputId(scopeRoot);
298 |       sourceName = "";
299 |       curNode = scopeRoot;
300 |       pushScope(scopeRoot);
301 | 
302 |       for (Node root : roots) {
303 |         Preconditions.checkState(root.getParent() == scopeRoot);
304 |         traverseBranch(root, scopeRoot);
305 |       }
306 | 
307 |       popScope();
308 |     } catch (Exception unexpectedException) {
309 |       throwUnexpectedException(unexpectedException);
310 |     }
311 |   }

120 |   public T process(AstNode node) {

174 |           return processBlock((Block) node);

179 |                                           node.getClass() +
180 |                                           " type: " +
181 |                                           Token.typeToName(node.getType()));
182 |         }

184 |         return processBreakStatement((BreakStatement) node);

273 |         } else {

278 |         }
279 |       case Token.WHILE:

281 |       case Token.WITH:

/**
     * Defines a symbol in the current scope.
     *
     * @param n the defining NAME or GETPROP or object literal key node.
     * @param parent the {@code n}'s parent.
     * @param variableName The name that this should be known by.
     * @param type the variable's type. It may be {@code null} if
     *     {@code inferred} is {@code true}.
     * @param inferred Whether the type is inferred or declared.
     */
Method code:
1090 |     void defineSlot(Node n, Node parent, String variableName,
1091 |         JSType type, boolean inferred) {
1092 |       Preconditions.checkArgument(!variableName.isEmpty());
1093 | 
1094 |       boolean isGlobalVar = n.isName() && scope.isGlobal();
1095 |       boolean shouldDeclareOnGlobalThis =
1096 |           isGlobalVar &&
1097 |           (parent.isVar() ||
1098 |            parent.isFunction());
1099 | 
1100 |       // If n is a property, then we should really declare it in the
1101 |       // scope where the root object appears. This helps out people
1102 |       // who declare "global" names in an anonymous namespace.
1103 |       Scope scopeToDeclareIn = scope;
1104 |       if (n.isGetProp() && !scope.isGlobal() &&
1105 |           isQnameRootedInGlobalScope(n)) {
1106 |         Scope globalScope = scope.getGlobalScope();
1107 | 
1108 |         // don't try to declare in the global scope if there's
1109 |         // already a symbol there with this name.
1110 |         if (!globalScope.isDeclared(variableName, false)) {
1111 |           scopeToDeclareIn = scope.getGlobalScope();
1112 |         }
1113 |       }
1114 | 
1115 |       // The input may be null if we are working with a AST snippet. So read
1116 |       // the extern info from the node.
1117 |       boolean isExtern = n.isFromExterns();
1118 |       Var newVar = null;
1119 | 
1120 |       // declared in closest scope?
1121 |       CompilerInput input = compiler.getInput(inputId);
1122 |       if (scopeToDeclareIn.isDeclared(variableName, false)) {
1123 |         Var oldVar = scopeToDeclareIn.getVar(variableName);
1124 |         newVar = validator.expectUndeclaredVariable(
1125 |             sourceName, input, n, parent, oldVar, variableName, type);
1126 |       } else {
1127 |         if (!inferred) {
1128 |           setDeferredType(n, type);
1129 |         }
1130 | 
1131 |         newVar =
1132 |           scopeToDeclareIn.declare(variableName, n, type, input, inferred);
1133 | 
1134 |         if (type instanceof EnumType) {
1135 |           Node initialValue = newVar.getInitialValue();
1136 |           boolean isValidValue = initialValue != null &&
1137 |               (initialValue.isObjectLit() ||
1138 |                initialValue.isQualifiedName());
1139 |           if (!isValidValue) {
1140 |             compiler.report(JSError.make(sourceName, n, ENUM_INITIALIZER));
1141 |           }
1142 |         }
1143 |       }
1144 | 
1145 |       // We need to do some additional work for constructors and interfaces.
1146 |       FunctionType fnType = JSType.toMaybeFunctionType(type);
1147 |       if (fnType != null &&
1148 |           // We don't want to look at empty function types.
1149 |           !type.isEmptyType()) {
1150 |         if ((fnType.isConstructor() || fnType.isInterface()) &&
1151 |             !fnType.equals(getNativeType(U2U_CONSTRUCTOR_TYPE))) {
1152 |           // Declare var.prototype in the scope chain.
1153 |           FunctionType superClassCtor = fnType.getSuperClassConstructor();
1154 |           ObjectType.Property prototypeSlot = fnType.getSlot("prototype");
1155 | 
1156 |           // When we declare the function prototype implicitly, we
1157 |           // want to make sure that the function and its prototype
1158 |           // are declared at the same node. We also want to make sure
1159 |           // that the if a symbol has both a Var and a JSType, they have
1160 |           // the same node.
1161 |           //
1162 |           // This consistency is helpful to users of SymbolTable,
1163 |           // because everything gets declared at the same place.
1164 |           prototypeSlot.setNode(n);
1165 | 
1166 |           String prototypeName = variableName + ".prototype";
1167 | 
1168 |           // There are some rare cases where the prototype will already
1169 |           // be declared. See TypedScopeCreatorTest#testBogusPrototypeInit.
1170 |           // Fortunately, other warnings will complain if this happens.
1171 |           Var prototypeVar = scopeToDeclareIn.getVar(prototypeName);
1172 |           if (prototypeVar != null && prototypeVar.scope == scopeToDeclareIn) {
1173 |             scopeToDeclareIn.undeclare(prototypeVar);
1174 |           }
1175 | 
1176 |           scopeToDeclareIn.declare(prototypeName,
1177 |               n, prototypeSlot.getType(), input,
1178 |               /* declared iff there's an explicit supertype */
1179 |               superClassCtor == null ||
1180 |               superClassCtor.getInstanceType().equals(
1181 |                   getNativeType(OBJECT_TYPE)));
1182 | 
1183 |           // Make sure the variable is initialized to something if
1184 |           // it constructs itself.
1185 |           if (newVar.getInitialValue() == null &&
1186 |               !isExtern &&
1187 |               // We want to make sure that when we declare a new instance
1188 |               // type (with @constructor) that there's actually a ctor for it.
1189 |               // This doesn't apply to structural constructors
1190 |               // (like function(new:Array). Checking the constructed
1191 |               // type against the variable name is a sufficient check for
1192 |               // this.
1193 |               variableName.equals(
1194 |                   fnType.getInstanceType().getReferenceName())) {
1195 |             compiler.report(
1196 |                 JSError.make(sourceName, n,
1197 |                     fnType.isConstructor() ?
1198 |                     CTOR_INITIALIZER : IFACE_INITIALIZER,
1199 |                     variableName));
1200 |           }
1201 |         }
1202 |       }
1203 | 
1204 |       if (shouldDeclareOnGlobalThis) {
1205 |         ObjectType globalThis =
1206 |             typeRegistry.getNativeObjectType(GLOBAL_THIS);
1207 |         if (inferred) {
1208 |           globalThis.defineInferredProperty(variableName,
1209 |               type == null ?
1210 |               getNativeType(JSTypeNative.NO_TYPE) :
1211 |               type,
1212 |               n);
1213 |         } else {
1214 |           globalThis.defineDeclaredProperty(variableName, type, n);
1215 |         }
1216 |       }
1217 | 
1218 |       if (isGlobalVar && "Window".equals(variableName)
1219 |           && type != null
1220 |           && type.isFunctionType()
1221 |           && type.isConstructor()) {
1222 |         FunctionType globalThisCtor =
1223 |             typeRegistry.getNativeObjectType(GLOBAL_THIS).getConstructor();
1224 |         globalThisCtor.getInstanceType().clearCachedValues();
1225 |         globalThisCtor.getPrototype().clearCachedValues();
1226 |         globalThisCtor
1227 |             .setPrototypeBasedOn((type.toMaybeFunctionType()).getInstanceType());
1228 |       }
1229 |     }

/**
     * Handle typedefs.
     * @param t The current traversal.
     * @param candidate A qualified name node.
     * @param info JSDoc comments.
     */
Method code:
1789 |     private void checkForTypedef(
1790 |         NodeTraversal t, Node candidate, JSDocInfo info) {
1791 |       if (info == null || !info.hasTypedefType()) {
1792 |         return;
1793 |       }
1794 | 
1795 |       String typedef = candidate.getQualifiedName();
1796 |       if (typedef == null) {
1797 |         return;
1798 |       }

1800 |       // TODO(nicksantos|user): This is a terrible, terrible hack
1801 |       // to bail out on recusive typedefs. We'll eventually need
1802 |       // to handle these properly.
1803 |       typeRegistry.declareType(typedef, getNativeType(UNKNOWN_TYPE));
1804 | 
1805 |       JSType realType = info.getTypedefType().evaluate(scope, typeRegistry);
1806 |       if (realType == null) {
1807 |         compiler.report(
1808 |             JSError.make(
1809 |                 t.getSourceName(), candidate, MALFORMED_TYPEDEF, typedef));
1810 |       }
1811 | 
1812 |       typeRegistry.overwriteDeclaredType(typedef, realType);
1813 |       if (candidate.isGetProp()) {
1814 |         defineSlot(candidate, candidate.getParent(),
1815 |             getNativeType(NO_TYPE), false);
1816 |       }
1817 |     }
